[SECTION: LHC_CASE_023]
Case: Crl. Misc.-Pre-arrest Bail-Under Section 498 Cr.PC 3115-B-26 (MUHAMMAD ISHFAQ ALIAS ISHTIAQ VS THE STATE ETC.).
Court: Lahore High Court, Lahore. Case No: Crl. Misc.-Pre-arrest Bail-Under Section 498 Cr.PC 3115-B-26. Title: Crl. Misc.-Pre-arrest Bail-Under Section 498 Cr.PC 3115-B-26 (MUHAMMAD ISHFAQ ALIAS ISHTIAQ VS THE STATE ETC.). Official Ref: 2026LHC5308. Author Judge: Mr. Justice Muhammad Usman Ghani Rashid Cheema. Judgment Date (as displayed): 24-08-2026. Record Type: FULL_TEXT_VERBATIM (complete judgment text extracted from the official court PDF). Source URL: https://sys.lhc.gov.pk/appjudgments/2026LHC5308.pdf. Verified: 2026-09-06 against the official court repository.

VERBATIM JUDGMENT TEXT (extracted from the official PDF at the Source URL; 5 pages; spelling, punctuation and lineation as in the PDF):

Form No: HCJD/C - 121
ORDER SHEET
IN THE LAHORE HIGH  C OURT
BAHAWALPUR BENCH, BAHAWALPUR
JUDICIAL DEPARTMENT
Case No:  Crl. Misc. No.  3115 - B of  2026
Muhammad Ishfaq @ Ishtiaq  V ersus  The State  and another
S.No. of  order/
Proceeding
Date of order/
Proceeding
Order with signature of Judge and that of
parties or counsel, where necessary .
19 . 08 . 2026
Mr.  Muhammad  Tariq  Mahmood ,  Advocate  with
petitioner .
Mr.  Javed  Iqbal  Bhayya,  Assistant  District  Public
Prosecutor alongwith Muhammad Abbas, SI.
Mr. Sohail Akhtar Alkara, Advocate for the complainant
alongwith complainant in person .
Through this  petition ,  under  s ection  498 Cr.P.C. ,  the
petitioner  seek s  pre - arrest bail in case FIR No. 130 / 2026 ,
dated  02 . 03 .2 026 ,  in respect of  an  offence  under  s ection  406
PPC , registered at  Police Station  City Haroonabad, District
Bahawalnagar .
2 .  At  the  very  outset,  learned  counsel  for  the
complainant  alongwith  complainant/Muneeb  Alam  states
that the matter between the parties has been sorted out of
Court and the complainant has no objection if ad - interim
pre - arrest bail of the petitioner is confirmed.  However, the
learned ADPP has vehemently opposed this settlement and
grant of b ail on basis thereof stating that offence of 406 PPC
is not compoundable in nature and such a statement by the
complainant does  not amount to compounding of offence u/s
345 Cr.P.C. Hence, he  requested  that this bail  petition  be
decided on merits and  dismis sed  in view of the facts of the
case.
3 .  Arguments heard and record perused with the able
assistance of the counsel for the parties.
Crl.Misc.No. 3115 - B of 202 6
4 .  F acts of this case, summarily, are that petitioner along
co - ac c used took  complainant’s motor car for temporary use
but did not return the same, rather sold it to same one else.
Hence, he committed willful breach of trust ,  thus ,  this FIR.
5 .  The above stance of the complainant brings to the fore
the significance of an amicable settlement in the matter of
bail, notwithstanding the non - compoundable character of the
offence.  Although an offence under section 406, PPC is non -
compoundable in nature , yet the factum of an amicable out -
of - Court settlement between the parties has consistently
been regarded as a relevant circumstance while considering
the question of bail, as such settlement tends to restore
harmony  between  the  parties  and  put  an  end  to  their
hostility.
6 .  Judicial notice has always been taken by the Courts
where  compromise / out of Court settlement was reached in
ceases  that were otherwise not compoundable. Reliance in
this regard is placed on the case titled as  “ Mukhtar Ahmad
and 3 others  vs. The State ”  ( 1999 P.Cr.L.J. 1107 ), wherein
paragraph No.4 reads as under:
“4. Unnatural offences as mentioned in section 377
of the Pakistan Penal Code do not find mention in
the table given in section 345, subsection (1) of the
Criminal Procedure Code  and, therefore, are not
compoundable. Similarly, section 12 of the Offence
of Zina (Enforcement of Hudood) Ordinance of
1979, (Ordinance No.VII of 1979) does not figure
in the table mentioned under section 345, Cr.P.C.
and, therefore, is not compoundable.  However, the
fact that the parties have themselves voluntarily
forgotten and forgiven a certain crime and have
entered into an outside Court settlement, may be
considered as a ground for the grant of bail in the
interest of justice and equity. If the comp lainant
party is no longer willing to prosecute a matter any
further then it is not for this Court or the Courts
subordinate to it to compel the parties to do so, as
the saying goes, "you can take the horse till the
wate r but you cannot make him drink .”
Crl.Misc.No. 3115 - B of 202 6
R eliance is  also  placed on  “ Sohail Aslam vs. The State
and  another ”  ( 2017  MLD  4 ),  wherein,  despite  the
allegations attracting non - bailable and non - compoundable
offences,  the  complainant  and  the  alleged  victim  had
voluntarily forgiven the accused and suppo rted his plea for
pre - arrest bail after an amicable settlement. This Court took
into  consideration  their  unequivocal  no - objection,  the
apology tendered by the accused and the restoration of
peaceful  relations  between  the  parties,  and  held  that  a
voluntary  out - of - Court  settlement,  though  incapable  of
converting  a  non - compoundable  offence  into  a
compoundable one, could nevertheless constitute a relevant
circumstance for grant of bail in the interest of justice and
equity. Consequently, the ad - interim pre - arre st bail granted
to the accused was confirmed.
Similar view has been taken by this Court in the cases
titled as  “ Muhammad Akram vs. The State ”  ( 1995 MLD
1826 ) and  “ Mst. Mussarat Elahi alias Bibi vs. The State ”
( 1997 P.Cr.L.J. 1193 ).
Another case, where compromise was effected in non -
compoundable  case  was  appreciated  by  this  Court  is
“ Shahid vs. The State and another ”  ( 2017 YLR Note 81 ),
where this Court  was pleased to note the following: -
“ Needless  to  say,  compromise  even  in  non -
comp oundable  offences  makes  a  crucial
circumstance and a redeeming feature which helps
the warring - parties come close to each other and
live  peacefully  onwards.  Let  non - compoundable
nature of the offences under sections 452, 354,
P.P.C. not frustrate their nob le intentions. ”
Crl.Misc.No. 3115 - B of 202 6
Most  importantly,  t he  Hon’ble  Supreme  Court  of
Pakistan in the case of  “ Ghulam Ali v. The State and
another ”  ( 1997 SCMR 1411 ) also considered the factum of
compromise in a non - compoundable offence and accepted
the appeal while reducing the sentence of the accused.
7 .  The principle that can safely be deduced from the
above judicial pronouncements is that,  notwithstanding the
non - compoundable  nature  of  an  offence,  an  amicable
settlement between the parties does not become altogether
inconsequential  while  considering  the  question  of  bail.
Though such settlement cannot amount to compounding of
the offence with in the contemplation of section 345, Cr.P.C.,
it may nevertheless be taken into consideration as a relevant
circumstance  in  the  exercise  of  judicial  discretion,
particularly where the complainant has voluntarily forgiven
the accused, does not wish to pursu e the matter and raises no
objection to the grant or confirmation of bail.
8 .  In the present case, the complainant has appeared
before this Court in person and categorically stated that the
dispute has been amicably resolved outside the Court and
that he h as no objection to the confirmation of ad - interim
pre - arrest bail already granted to the petitioner. Therefore,
while  keeping  the  non - compoundable  character  of  the
offence intact and without expressing any opinion on the
merits of the case, this Court hold s that the subsequent
settlement  between  the parties  and  the unequivocal no -
objection extended by the complainant constitute relevant
circumstances which can legitimately be taken into account
for confirmation of the petitioner's pre - arrest bail.
9 .  In vie w of the above - mentioned circumstances and
without touching the merits of the case, this petition is
allowed  and the ad - interim pre - arrest bail already granted to
Crl.Misc.No. 3115 - B of 202 6
the petitioner is  confirmed , subject to his furnishing fresh
bail bonds in the sum of Rs.1,00 ,000/ -  (Rupees one lac only)
with one surety in the like amount to the satisfaction of the
trial Court.
( Muhammad Usman Ghani Rashid Cheema )
Judge
Waqas*
Approved for Reporting

[SECTION: LHC_CASE_024]
Case: Crl. Misc. 33563/26 (Muhammad Abbas Vs The State etc).
Court: Lahore High Court, Lahore. Case No: Crl. Misc. 33563/26. Title: Crl. Misc. 33563/26 (Muhammad Abbas Vs The State etc). Official Ref: 2026LHC5300. Author Judge: Mr. Justice Muhammad Amjad Pervaiz. Judgment Date (as displayed): 24-08-2026. Record Type: FULL_TEXT_VERBATIM (complete judgment text extracted from the official court PDF). Source URL: https://sys.lhc.gov.pk/appjudgments/2026LHC5300.pdf. Verified: 2026-09-06 against the official court repository.

VERBATIM JUDGMENT TEXT (extracted from the official PDF at the Source URL; 8 pages; spelling, punctuation and lineation as in the PDF):

Form No. HCJD/C - 121
ORDER SHEET
IN THE LAHORE HIGH COURT  LAHORE
JUDICIAL DEPARTMENT
Crl.Misc.No.33563-B of 2026
Muhammad Abbas vs. The State and another.
Sr. No. of
order/
proceedings
Date of
order/
proceedings.
Order with signatures of Judge, and that of parties or
counsel, where necessary.
20.08.2026  M/s Ijaz Ahmad Janjua and Saleem Ahmad Khan
Advocates for the petitioner.
Ch.Usman Ghani, Assistant Attorney General with
Ayesha Masood SI/FIA.
Complainant with Mr.Junaid Sarvar, Advocate
Through the instant petition under section 497
Cr.P.C., the petitioner seeks bail after arrest in case FIR
No.1047 dated 31.12.2025 for  offences under sections
17, 22 of the Emigration Ordinance, 1979  (hereinafter
Ordinance) registered at Police Station FIA/AHTC, Lahore.
2.  As per the contents of the FIR, the prosecution story,
precisely, is that in the year 2024, the petitioner, along with his
co-accused, allegedly fleeced an amount of Rs.9,00,000/- from
the  complainant,  partly  in  cash  and  partly  through  bank
transactions, on the pretext of sending her abroad, i.e. to
Muscat, Oman, for employment. However, neither was she
sent abroad nor was the amount so received returned to her.
3.  Arguments heard. Record perused.
4.  Before I proceed to examine the case on merits, I am
constrained to first advert to a legal proposition which has
arisen in this case during the course of arguments.
5.  Learned counsel for the petitioner contends that the
alternative punishments have been prescribed for offences
under sections 17 and 22 of the Ordinance, hence, at bail stage
lesser sentence is to be considered. He has placed reliance on
Crl.Misc.No.33563-B of 2026
“ Messrs Askari Leasing Limited through Branch Manager vs.
Rana Muhammad Asif and 2 others ”  ( 2005 P.Cr.L.J 1265 ),
“ Muhammad Mustaqeem vs. The State ”  ( 2004 YLR 68 ) and
“ Hafiz Ahmad Khan vs. The State ”  ( 1988 P Cr.LJ 338 ),
“ Sohail Younas vs. The State and others ”  ( 2015 P. Crl.LJ563 )
and  “ Sohaib Ali Hashmat vs. The State and another ”  ( 2025
MLD 925 ).
While  concluding  his  arguments,  learned  counsel
submitted  that  in  cases  of  alternative  punishment  i.e.
imprisonment  “or with fine”, the offence charged does not
attract the mischief of prohibitory clause in terms of section
497 Cr.P.C.
6.  On the other hand, learned Law Officer submits that
the above arguments are untenable in view of the principle
enunciated by the Supreme Court of Pakistan in  Jabran’s
case  ( 2025 SCMR 199 ). He further submits that in view of the
amendments  introduced  into  various  provisions  of  the
Ordinance, including sections 17 and 22, the principle of
alternative punishment is no more available in this case.
7.  At  this  stage  it  is  appropriate  to  reproduce  ACT
No.VIII of 2025 (hereinafter Amendment Act), through which
amendments have been brought into the Ordinance:-
“ Whereas  it is expedient further to amend the Emigration
Ordinance, 1979(XVIII of 1979) for the purposes and in the
manner hereinafter appearing;
It is hereby enacted as follows:-
1.  Short titled and commencement.  (1) This Act shall be
called the Emigration (Amendment)Act, 2025
2.  It shall come into force at once . (emphasis provided)
2. Amendment of Section 17, Ordinance XVIII of 1979 .
In the Emigration Ordinance, 1979 (XVIII of 1979), hereinafter
referred to as the said Ordinance,  in section 17, in sub-section
(1), for the expression  “, or  with fine, or with both.” The
words “and with fine up to one million rupees” shall be
substituted.  (underlining has been provided for emphasis)
Crl.Misc.No.33563-B of 2026
3.  Amendment of section 18, Ordinance XVIII of 1979.
In the said Ordinance,, in section 18, for the expression “, or
with fine, or with both”, the words “ and with fine up to two
million rupees” shall be substituted.
4 .  Amendment of section 22, Ordinance XVIII of 1979 .
In the said ordinance, in section 22,  for the expression “, or
with fine, or with both” , the words “  and with fine up to two
million rupees”  shall be substituted. (emphasis supplied)
As per sub-section 2 of section 1, the Amendment Act
has come into force at once and without retrospective effect.
8.  The simple question involved herein is as to whether
or not the provisions of the Amendment Act are applicable in
this case wherein the occurrence has taken place prior to its
promulgation.
Bare perusal of sections 2, 3 and 4 of the Amendment
Act makes it clear that the punishment now prescribed is  not
alternative one  as the expression  “ or with fine ” has been
substituted with the word  “ and ” so it is clear that the amended
provisions have authorized punishment for an offence by a
penalty greater than or of different kind from, the penalty
prescribed by law earlier. In this view of the matter, the above
substitution  attracts  the  protection  against  retrospective
punishment guaranteed as fundamental right vide Article 12
Chapter 1 Part II of the Constitution of Islamic Republic of
Pakistan, 1973 (the Constitution). For ready reference same is
reproduced hereunder:-
“  Protection against retrospective punishment.
(1)  No law shall authorize the punishment of a person:__
(a)  for an act or omission that was not punishable by law at the time
of the act or omission; or
(b)  for an offence by a penalty greater than, or of a kind different
from, the penalty prescribed by law for that offence at the time
the offence was committed .
The scope of Article 12 of our Constitution and its principle
has been matter of discussion by courts and jurists across
Crl.Misc.No.33563-B of 2026
jurisdictions since ages, however, for the sake of brevity, I
would hasten to rely upon recent pronouncement by Supreme
Court of Pakistan in  “ Anwar Saif Ullah Khan’s  case (2026
SCP 129) is being reproduced hereunder:-
7. Article 12 is not a rule of convenience; it is a substantive
fundamental right enshrined in Chapter I of Part II of the
Constitution. It embodies the ancient maxim “ nullum crimen,
nulla poena sine lege praevia ”, no crime and no punishment
without prior law. It also applies in a situation where, though it
was previously a crime, but the punishment under the newly
notified enactment was greater than the one provided in the
previous  law.  As  a  non-derogable  guarantee,  it  prohibits
retrospective criminality and operates as an absolute bar on
both  the legislature and the courts. This  principle is  also
reflected in Islamic jurisprudence. Allah Almighty states in the
Qur’an: “ And We would never punish a people until We have
sent a messenger to warn them” (17:15, Surah Al -Isra) . This
establishes  a  foundational  rule  of  justice:  punishment  is
contingent upon prior communication of the law. Accordingly,
retrospective punishment is  impermissible, as liability of a
specific charge arises only after the law has been enacted and
duly conveyed. In practical terms, this means that individuals
cannot be held accountable for actions committed before a
relevant  prohibition  or  obligation  was  imposed  and
consequently made known to them. This aligns with the broader
legal maxim that there is no culpability without prior notice,
typically ensured through publication in the official gazette,
thereby  safeguarding  fairness  and  preventing  arbitrary
punishment. Although Section 2 of the NAO, 1999 purports to
operate retrospectively from 01.01.1985, the decision of this
Court in  Asfandyar Wali v. Federation of Pakistan (PLD 2001
SC  607)  establishes  that  Article  12  does  not  curtail  the
competence  of  the  Legislature  to  enact  laws  having
retrospective effect for all purposes, but it does prohibit, in the
clearest possible terms, the imposition of  criminal liability
where, at the time of the act or omission, no offence or penalty
existed in law, or where the later law seeks to impose a
punishment greater in degree or different in kind from that
which  was  prescribed  when  the  offence  was  allegedly
committed.  The  distinction  is  constitutional,  not  merely
technical. A legislature may declare the general applicability of
a statute to earlier periods; it may not, however, do so where
the effect would be to fasten criminal guilt retrospectively. The
moment the retrospective clause of a penal statute encounters
the Article 12 barrier, the clause must yield and the barrier
must prevail.”
The above interpretation has been made by the Supreme Court
of Pakistan in a Criminal Review Petition. Now the question is
as to whether or not, the above principle is applicable in bail
matters, the same has been answered in  “ Sajjad Ha ider’s case
Crl.Misc.No.33563-B of 2026
(2026  SCP  54),  relevant  portion  whereof  is  reproduced
hereunder:-
“W e have heard the learned counsels for the parties and
gone through the record in depth. In order to avoid
unnecessary  repetition,  we  have  incorporated  and
addressed  the  submissions  and  contentions  raised
during the course of arguments throughout the ensuing
discussion and analysis within this judgment. At the very
outset, we have noted that the main offence for which
the petitioners have been nominated is section 23 of the
Act of 1947 where the offence is currently punishable
with  rigorous  imprisonment  for  a  term  which  may
extend to five years or with fine or with both. It is
relevant to state here that previously the punishment for
the said offence was simple imprisonment extendable up
to two years or fine or both and the same was amended
in the year 2020 through the Act No. X of 2020 w.e.f.
27.02.2020. The date of occurrence as per FIR is year
2007-2015 which is before the ibid amendment of 2020.
As per the protections guaranteed by the Article 12 of
the Constitution of Islamic Republic of Pakistan, 1973
(“ The  Constitution ”),  no  law  shall  authorize  the
punishment of a person for an offence by a penalty
greater than, or of' a kind different from, the penalty
prescribed by law for that offence at the time the offence
was  committed.  At  a  glance,  protection  against
retrospective punishment as provided in Article 12 of the
Constitution might appear as a notion dealing with
procedural requirement, however it not as such. Article
12 of the Constitution does not state any procedural
requirement of ordinary course rather it is one of the
fundamental rights and highly guarded substantive right
which  is  not  to  be  taken  lightly.  This  doctrine
concerning ex post facto laws is well recognized in
almost all jurisdictions with respect to administration of
criminal  justice.  As  per  well  recognized  legal
encyclopedia  of  American  law  i.e.  Corpus  Juris
Secundum!, an ex post facto law is one which makes
criminal and punishes an act which was done before the
passage of the law and which was innocent when done,
aggravates a crime or makes it greater than it was when
committed,  changes  the  punishment  and  inflicts  a
greater punishment than was prescribed when the crime
was committed, or alters the legal rules of evidence and
receives less or different testimony than was required to
convict at the time the offence was committed. Further,
an ex post facto law may be one which, assuming to
regulate civil rights and remedies only, in effect imposes
a penalty or the deprivation of a right for something
which, when done, was lawful, deprives persons accused
of crime of some lawful protection or defence previously
available to them, such as the protection of a former
conviction or acquittal, or of a proclamation of amnesty,
or,  generally,  in  relation  to  the  offence  or  its
consequences, alters the situation of an accused to his
material disadvantage. Protection against such penal
legislation is also provided by the Convention for the
Crl.Misc.No.33563-B of 2026
Protection  of  Human  Rights  and  Fundamental
Freedoms which was signed by members of the Council
of Europe in 1950, and later ratified by the United
Kingdom. The convictions and sentences to be recorded
in the criminal jurisdiction under ex post facto law are
also prohibited under Article 12 of the Constitution and
well recognized by this court in its various judgments.
The benefit of the said interpretation must also be
extended to an accused not only at the stage of trial but
also at the stage of bail . Clearly, in the instant case, the
offence was allegedly committed at the time between
years 2007 and 2015, therefore, the punishment for the
offence under section 23 ibid would be considered as
two years or fine or both”
9.  In view of the above enunciation of principle of law,
there is no manner of doubt that the constitutional protection
against retrospective punishment at the anvil of Article 12 of
the Constitution is available to the petitioner. Hence, the
provisions of Amendment Act are not applicable in this case
as the occurrence took place in the year 2024.
10.  Now I proceed to examine the facts and merits of the
case tentatively to find out as to whether any case of bail after
arrest is made out or not. It evinces that the F.I.R. in this case
has been lodged with a delay of about one year. No time, date
and place of alleged payment is mentioned in the F.I.R. There
is no documentary proof of any payment to the petitioner. As
per affidavit of Nasir Iqbal who is a prosecution witness, the
alleged amount was paid to co-accused Iftikhar (since P.O).
The  petitioner  was  arrested  on  03.03.2026.  His  physical
custody was remanded to the investigating officer but no
incriminating material has been recovered from him. There is
no evidence that the petitioner is beneficiary, so the question
of guilt of the petitioner qua the alleged occurrence, at this
stage, calls for further probe within the ambit of sub-section 2
of section 497 Cr.P.C. The investigation to the extent of the
petitioner is complete and he is no more required for further
investigation. His further incarceration would not serve any
useful purpose rather, the same would amount to punishment
before trial. It is settled law that bail cannot be withheld as
Crl.Misc.No.33563-B of 2026
punishment.  Reliance in this regard is placed upon “ Manzoor
and 4 other v. State ” ( PLD 1972 SC 81 ), relevant portion
whereof is reproduced hereunder:-
“ It is important to remember that bail is not to be
withheld as a punishment. There is no legal or moral
compulsion to keep people in jail merely on the
allegation  that  they  have  committed  offences
punishable  with  death  or  transportation,  unless
reasonable grounds appear to exist to disclose their
complicity.  The  ultimate  conviction  and
incarceration  of  a  guilty  person  can  repair  the
wrong caused by a mistaken relief of interim bail
granted to him, but no satisfactory reparation can be
offered  to  an  innocent  man  for  his  unjustified
incarceration at any stage of the case albeit his
acquittal in the long run.”
11.  The  object  of  bail  has  been  elaborated  by  the
Hon’ble Supreme Court of Pakistan in  Khawaja Salman ’s
case  ( PLD 2020 SC 456 ), relevant portion whereof is
reproduced hereunder:-
“ While dealing with the question of whether or not bail is to be
granted to an accused, it has to be kept in mind that the object
of bail is to secure the attendance of the accused at the trial.
The object is neither punitive nor preventive. Imprisonment of a
person and deprivation of his liberty, cannot be described other
than, being a punishment, unless no less restrictive alternative
is available to ensure that the accused will stand his trial when
called upon to  do so. All  civilized societies recognize the
principle that punishment comes only after conviction, and the
presumption of innocence subsist with the accused, till he is
handed  down  punishment  after  trial.  It  hardly  needs  any
reiteration that the detention either pre-trial or during trial
causes great hardship.”
12.  Since the bail in this case has been granted in terms of
sub-section 2 of Section 497 Cr.P.C. I beg to be pardoned for
venturing the opinion as to whether or not a case of alternative
punishments would attract the mischief of prohibitory clause.
13.  For what has been discussed above, this petition is
allowed and the petitioner is admitted to post arrest bail
subject to his furnishing bail bonds in the sum of Rs.2,00,000/-
with one surety in the like amount to the satisfaction of the
learned trial court.
Crl.Misc.No.33563-B of 2026
14.  Before parting with this order, it is clarified that if the
petitioner or anyone else acting on his behalf, in any way,
delays the conclusion of the trial, the other side (State or the
complainant)  shall  be  at  liberty  to  file  a  petition  for
cancellation of bail under Section 497(5) Cr.P.C. before the
learned trial court.
(Muhammad Amjad Pervaiz)
Judge
Approved for reporting.
Judge
M.J.Mehmood

[SECTION: LHC_CASE_025]
Case: Service 6323/26 (Shafique Khushnood Vs MS General Hospital etc.).
Court: Lahore High Court, Lahore. Case No: Service 6323/26. Title: Service 6323/26 (Shafique Khushnood Vs MS General Hospital etc.). Official Ref: 2026LHC5289. Author Judge: Mr. Justice Malik Muhammad Awais Khalid. Judgment Date (as displayed): 19-08-2026. Record Type: FULL_TEXT_VERBATIM (complete judgment text extracted from the official court PDF). Source URL: https://sys.lhc.gov.pk/appjudgments/2026LHC5289.pdf. Verified: 2026-09-06 against the official court repository.

VERBATIM JUDGMENT TEXT (extracted from the official PDF at the Source URL; 11 pages; spelling, punctuation and lineation as in the PDF):

Stereo. H C J D A 38
Judgment Sheet
IN THE  LAHORE HIGH COURT,  LAHORE
JUDICIAL DEPARTMENT
W.P .  No. 6323  of  20 2 6
Shafique Khushnood .
Vs.
Medical Superintendent ,  Lahore  General Hospital Lahore  and
two others .
JUDGMENT
Date of Hearing  20 . 5 .2026
Petitioner  by  M/s  Sardar Ali Kamboh  and Rai Riaz
Ahmad Kharal ,  Advocate s .
Respondent s  by  Ch. Awais Ahmad Qazi, Addl. Advocate
General  &  Mr.  Faisal  Nawaz,  Legal
Advisor for respondents.
MALIK  MUHAMMAD  AWAIS  KHALID ,  J .  T he
petitioner has filed  instant  constitutional  petition  with the
following prayer: -
“ It is, therefore, most respectfully prayed that
this writ petition may kindly be accepted and
the  impugned  office  order  No.PF/
468586 4 /LGH dated 24.7.2024 issued by  the
respondent No.2/Addl: Medical Sup erintendent,
Lahore  General  Hospital,  Lahore  and
impugned order No.PF/63503 dated 19.9.2024
passed  by  respondent  No.1/Medical
Superintendent,  Lahore  General  Hospital,
Lahore  may  kindly  be  set  aside  and  the
petitioner may graciously be reinstated in the
ser vice with all back benefits, in the interest of
justice.
2.  Succinctly,  the  facts  of  the  case  are  that
petitioner  was  serving  as  Security  Guard  (BS -
01/Permanent Basis) BOM created post in  Lahore  General
Hospital,  Lahore  and  has  been  performing  his  duties
honestly and  diligently .  Thereafter,  show  cause  notice
W.P .  No. 6323 / 20 2 6  2
No.PF/45035/LGH  dated  15.7.2024  was  issued  to  th e
petitioner  by  respondent  No.2  while  the  inquiry  was
dispensed with.  The petitioner submitted reply wherein he
denied the allegations leveled aga inst him but respondent
No.2 without conducting regular inquiry and providing
opportunity of hearing, terminated his service vide order
dated 24.7.2024. Feeling aggrieved, petitioner preferred
departmental appeal before respondent No.1 which  was
dismi ssed  vide order dated 19.9.2024.  The  appeal filed by
the  petitioner  before Punjab Service Tribunal, Lahor e, was
dismissed being not  appropriate forum  vide order dated
13.1.2026.  Hence, this writ petition.
3.  Learned counsel for the petitioner submits that
im pugned orders have been passed unlawfully and without
mentioning any reason  therein  by the respondents; the
word termination is alien to law; conducting of regular
inquiry was dispensed with by the responden t - department
against Article 10A of the Constitut ion of the Is lamic
Republic of Pakistan 1973 (The Constitution).
4.  Learned  Law Officer  and learned Legal Advisor
for respondent - department have  opposed the petition on
the ground that impugned orders have been passed quite in
accordance with law  and  justifiable reasons.
5 .  Arguments heard. Record perused.
6 .  The  allegation  leveled  against the petitioner was
that he was c aught red handed while stealing A.C. pipes in
W.P .  No. 6323 / 20 2 6  3
ortho  ward  of  Lahore  General  Hospital,  Lahore  on
0 1.5.2024 at 4:30 A.M.  and remaine d absent  from duty
w.e.f. 15.6.2024  to 22.7.2024. Show cause notice was
issued  to  the  petitioner  under  section  7(b)  read  with
Sections  5(1)(a)  of  Punjab  Employees  Efficiency
Discipline & Accountability Act, 2006  ( the  Act)  with the
direction to submit reply. The petitioner submitted his
reply to the show cause  notice  with the contention that at
about 4:30  a.m.  night  security  supervisor called  him  that
towards ortho ward someone was cutting A.C. pipe, he
visited the s pot but  no body was found there ;  he was not
absent from  duty.  Learned counsel for the petitioner took
the stance that inquiry against the petitioner was dispensed
with  without  mentioning  any reason.  Perusal  of show
cause notice  depicts that  no  cogent and justifia ble  reason
has been  enunciated  therein for dispensing with inquiry.
Relevant portion is reproduced as under: -
“AND WHEREAS, the undersigned is of the
opinion  that  it  is  not  necessary  to  hold  an
inquiry into the  matter in view of the provision
contained in section 5(1) (a)  of the  PEEDA  Act
2006,  therefore, proposed to proceed against
you under Section 7(b) read with section 5(1)(a)
of P EEDA Act, 2006 may not be imposed upon
you. ”
On  the face of it ,  no reason has been mentioned by the
competent authority  in the show cause notice  to dispense
with the inquiry but has proceeded against the petitioner
only on the basis of opinion which is  unwarranted under
law. With regard to absence of the petitioner, l earned
W.P .  No. 6323 / 20 2 6  4
counsel for the petitioner has produced copy of attendance
register for the months of June and July 2024 which is
apparently  showing attendance of the petitioner on most of
the dates.  Complaint was filed against the petitioner by
night supervisor Ra na Zeeshan on the pretext that there
was theft of A.C. pipe of ortho ward of the hospital on
1.5.2024. Learned counsel for the petitioner submits that
incident took place on 1.5.2024 but show cause notice was
issued against the petitioner on 15.7.2024 with  the delay of
more than two months.  It is important to note w hen  the
petitioner has denied the allegations levelled against him,
right of confrontation is provided under Articles 4 & 10A
of the  Constitution  and further u/s 10 of  the Act , right of
cross - exa mination is  available  to the petitioner  to prove his
innocence but denied arbitrarily .  Authenticity  or otherwise
of  the  disputed  facts  cannot  be  considered  without
conducting a  fair  regular inquiry.  Hon’ble Supreme Court
in  case s  of  Arbab  and  others  Vs.  Province  of  Sindh
through Secretary Health Department and others  ( 2026
SCMR  300 )  and  The Vice - President (Admin.), National
Bank of Pakistan and others Vs. Basharat Ali and others
( 1996 SCMR 201)  held that  paramount object of framing
of the  rules and enactment to provide a fair and reasonable
opportunity to an employee to defend himself against the
charge brought against him by his employer in order to
ensure adherence of the principles of natural justice.  The
W.P .  No. 6323 / 20 2 6  5
Hon’ble Supreme Court of Pakist an in the case of  Saad
Salam Ansari Vs. Chief Justice of Sindh  High  Court,
Karachi through Registrar  (2007 SCMR 1726)  held as
under: -
“The appellant has been subjected to a
major  penalty  terminating  his  service
prematurely. The spirit of the law namely
PEE DA Act, 2006 is that such terminal
punitive  action  against  a  civil  servant
should be taken after an inquiry has been
conducted. In the present case, whether the
appellant had a satisfactory explanation for
his absence for the period mentioned in the
show - c ause - notice has not been attended
by the respondents. It is a matter upon
which an opportunity to record evidence
ought to have been granted. Likewise, the
availability  of  documentary  evidence,  if
any,  with  the  respondents  that  justified
dispensation of an  inquiry ought to have
been disclosed/confronted to the appellant.
In the absence thereof, we find that the
impugned  punishment  has  been  imposed
upon the appellant without complying with
the provisions of the law.”
The  allegation  of  mis - conduct  levelled  against  the
delinquent could not be proved without conducting
regular inquiry especially when major  penalty  is going
to  be  imposed.  The  purpose  for  initiation  of
disciplinary proceeding is to find out whether charges
of misconduct are proved or not and in case his guilt is
established, what action should be taken against him
under  the  applicable  rules  which  may  include  the
imposition of penalties minor or major. Reliance is
placed on the case of  Chief Engineer, Gujranw ala
Electric Power Company (GEPCO) Gujranwala Vs.
Khalid  Mehmood  and  others  (2023  SCMR  291)
wherein it has been held as under: -
W.P .  No. 6323 / 20 2 6  6
“ The  allegations  of  dishonesty,
corruption  and  negligence  levelled
against the respondent No.1 could not be
proved  without  regul ar  inquiry.  The
astuteness  of  triggering  of  disciplinary
proceedings by the employer is to find out
whether  the  charges  of  misconduct
levelled against the delinquent are proved
or  not  and,  in  case  his  guilt  is
established, what action should be taken
again st him under the applicable Service
laws which may include the imposition of
minor  or  major  penalties.  There  is  no
rigid or definitive rule that in each and
every  case  after  issuing  show  cause
notice  a  regular  inquiry  should  be
conducted, but if the depart ment aspires
to dispense with the regular inquiry due
to  some  compelling  circumstances  or
exigency, then justifiable reasons should
be assigned in writing before dispensing
with the regular inquiry. No doubt if a
charge is set up or stems from admitted
doc uments,  no  full - fledged  regular
inquiry  is  obligatory,  but  if  the
allegations  are  based  on  disputed
questions  of  facts,  then  obviously  the
employee  cannot  be  denied  a  right  of
regular  inquiry,  specifically  where  the
allegations  cannot  be  resolved  without
l eading  evidence  and  providing  a  fair
opportunity  to  the  parties  to  cross -
examine the witnesses. ”
Further reliance can safely be placed on the case of
Naseeb Khan Vs. Divisional Superintendent, Pakistan
Railways, Lahore and another  (2008 SCMR 1369)
where in  it has been observed as follows: -
“ In case of imposing a major penalty, the
principle of natural justice requires that a
regular enquiry is to be conducted in the
matter  and  opportunity  of  defence  and
personal hearing is to be provided to the
civil servan t proceeded against as held by
this  Court  in  the  case  of  Pakistan
International  Airlines Corporation  v. Ms.
Shaista  Naheed  2004  SCMR  316  and
Inspector - General of Police, Karachi and
2 others v. Shafqat Mehmood 2003 SCMR
2007.
6.  Keeping  in  v iew  the  facts  and
circumstances of the case, we find that
petitioner has been condemned unheard
and  major  penalty  of  dismissal  from
service  has  been  imposed  upon  him
W.P .  No. 6323 / 20 2 6  7
without  adopting  the  required  and
mandatory  procedure,  resulting  in
manifest injustice. ”
T he  allegation s  levelled  in  the  show  cause  notice
issued against the petitioner on the face of it could not
be proved against the petitioner without proper inquiry
and  without  providing  an  opportunity  to  adduce
evidence not only to the complainant but also  to the
delinquent employee enabling him to defend against
the charges, produce witnes s in order to prove his
innocence  as well as to avail the right  to cross - examine
the witnesses  produced against him.
7.  It is also pertinent  to mention  that  in this
matt er  the competent authority imposed major penalty
of  termination  from  service  upon  the  petitioner
whereas disciplinary proceedings were initiated under
the Act and  show cause notice  was also issued under
the said Act.  The impugned order  of  the termination  of
the petitioner  from service  is alien to  the  Act. The
Section 4  of  the  Act  de scribes  the  penalties  which is
reproduced as under: -
“ 4.  Penalties. -  (1)  The  competent  authority  may,
notwithstanding anything contained in any law or the
terms and conditions of service of the accused, by an
order in writing, impose one or more of the following
penalties, namely: -
a) Minor penalties -
(i)  censure;
(ii)  withholding  of  increment  or
increments,  for  a  specific
period, subject to a maximum
of five years;
W.P .  No. 6323 / 20 2 6  8
(iii)  (iii)  fine  not  exceeding  basic
pay of one month;
(iv)  (iv) reduction to a lower stage
or stages in pay scale, subject
to a maximum of five stages;
and
(v)  wit hholding of promotion for a
specific  period,  subject  to  a
maximum  of  five  years;
provided that this period shall
be counted from the date when
a person junior to the accused
is  considered  for  promotion
and  is  promoted  on  regular
basis for the first time;
(b) Major penalties  –
(i)  recovery from pay, pension or
any other amount payable to
the  accused,  the  whole  or  a
part  of  any  pecuniary  loss
caused to the Government or
the organization in  which he
was  employed,  and  if  the
amount  due  from  any  such
person  c annot  be  wholly
recovered  from  the  pay,
pension or any other amount
payable to him, such amount
shall  be recovered  under the
law for the time being in force;
(ii)  reduction to a lower post and
pay scale from the substantive
or regular post for a specific
period subject to a maximum of
five years;
(iii)  forfeiture of past service for a
specific  period  subject  to  a
maximum of five years;
(iv)  compulsory retirement;
(v)  removal from service; and
(vi)  dismissal from service; and
(c)  Penalties after retire ment -
(i)  withholding of pension or any
part thereof; (ii) withdrawing
of pension or any part thereof;
and (iii) recovery from pension
or any other amount payable to
the accused, of pecuniary loss
caused to Government or the
organization in which he was
employed,  an d  if  the  amount
due  from  any  such  person
cannot  be  wholly  recovered
from the pension or any other
amount payable to him, such
amount  shall  be  recovered
under the law for the time being
in force.
(ii)  Dismissal  from  service  under
this  Ac t  shall  disqualify  the
employee  for  future
W.P .  No. 6323 / 20 2 6  9
employment  under  the
Government  or  under  any
organization  to  which  the
pr ovisions  of  this  Act  apply.
PEED A Act, 2006
(iii )  Any penalty under this Act shall
not  absolve  an  employee  or
accused from  liability to  any
punishment to  which he may be
liable for an offence, under any
law, committed by him while in
service.
Perusal of section ibid reveals that word  ‘ termination ’
is not  included  in the Act rather ,  word dismiss , remove
or  compulsory  retirement  has  been  provided .  The
imposi tion  of  major  penalty  of  ‘ termination  from
service’  is void being absent in the list pro vided  by the
Act ,  as show cause notice was issued to the petitioner
under section 7 of the Act .
8.  The principles of natural justice require that
delinquent should be given fair opportunity to contest
charges levelled against him before he is found guilty.
No person is unjustly penalized without being given
opportunity  to present his complete defence, t herefore,
impugned  orders  are  against  the  principle  of  due
process and transparency.  Reliance is placed on the
case  of  The  Government  of  Balochistan,  through
Secretary Energy Department, Quetta and another Vs.
Muhammad Yasir  (2025 SCMR 367)  wherein it has
been observed as follows:
“This action was in sheer contravention
of Article 10 - A of the Constitution of the
Islamic  Republic  of  Pakistan,  1973
("Constitution"), and lacked due process
of law. To enjoy the protection of law
and to be treated in accordance  with the
W.P .  No. 6323 / 20 2 6  10
law is a basic fundamental right of every
citizen within the precincts ad confines of
Article 4 of the Constitution which, in
fact, assimilates the doctrine of equal
protection of law and accentuates that no
action detrimental to life and liberty  can
be taken without due process.”
Reliance is  also  placed on the case of  Government of
Khyber Pakhtunkhwa through  Secretary  Elementary
and Secondary, Education Department Peshawar and
others  Vs.  Aurangzeb  (Ex - Pr imary  School  Head
Teacher) GPS W anna  Khel Tehsil Takhtbhai District
Mardan (deceased through L.Rs.  (2025 SCMR 40)
wherein it has been held as under: -
“In  our  considered  view,  the
allegations  jotted  down  in  the  show
cause notice, on the face of it, could not
be proved without proper inquiry a nd
without  providing  an  opportunity  to
adduce  evidence  not  only  to  the
complainants but also to the delinquent
employee for him to be able to defend
himself  against  the  charges,  produce
witnesses,  if  any,  to  prove  his
innocence, as well as avail the right  to
cross - examine the witnesses adducing
against  him.  On  the  contrary,  the
competent authority imposed the major
penalty of compulsory retirement from
service upon the delinquent employee
on the basis of a show cause notice
alone, without following the prin ciple
of  natural  justice  and  due  process
enunciated under the law.”
The  appellate authority also passed the  impugned  order
in  mechanical  manner  hence  arbitrary  in  nature .
Respondents  in a stereotype and  perfunctory  manner
proceeded against the petitioner and imposed him major
penalty  of  termination  from  service  which  is  not
warranted under law .  While  dispensing with the regular
W.P .  No. 6323 / 20 2 6  11
inquiry,  logical  reasons  must  be  mentioned  by  the
competent authority but in this case inq uiry has been
dispensed with in  a  whimsical  manner .  The punishment
awarded to the petitioner is in violation of Article s  4  &
10 - A of the Constitution and Section 4 of the Act.  The
impugned order s suffer  from illegality  hence,  calling for
interference by th is Court.
9 .  As  a  sequel  of  above  discussion ,  impugned
orders  are  set aside and the petitioner is reinstated into
service.  The competent authority may  order to  conduct
a  regular  i nquiry on the allegations level ed against the
petitioner ,  under law and  is  directed to decide the issue
of back benefits of the petitioner.
10 .  The  upshot of above discussion,  instant writ
petition is  partly allowed  in above terms.
( Malik Muhammad Awais Khalid )
JUDGE
Approved for reporting.
Judge
Naeem*

[SECTION: LHC_CASE_026]
Case: Crl. Misc.-Post-arrest Bail 5827-B-26 (MUHAMMAD AKHTAR & 1 OTHER VS STATE ETC ).
Court: Lahore High Court, Lahore. Case No: Crl. Misc.-Post-arrest Bail 5827-B-26. Title: Crl. Misc.-Post-arrest Bail 5827-B-26 (MUHAMMAD AKHTAR & 1 OTHER VS STATE ETC ). Official Ref: 2026LHC5264. Author Judge: Mr. Justice Syed Farhad Ali Shah. Judgment Date (as displayed): 18-08-2026. Record Type: FULL_TEXT_VERBATIM (complete judgment text extracted from the official court PDF). Source URL: https://sys.lhc.gov.pk/appjudgments/2026LHC5264.pdf. Verified: 2026-09-06 against the official court repository.

VERBATIM JUDGMENT TEXT (extracted from the official PDF at the Source URL; 4 pages; spelling, punctuation and lineation as in the PDF):

Form No. HCJD/C - 121
ORDER SHEET
IN THE LAHORE HIGH COURT
MULTAN BENCH MULTAN
JUDICIAL DEPARTMENT
Crl.Misc. No.5827 - B of 2026
Muhammad Akhta r & another. Vs. The State & another.
Sr. No. of
order/
proceedings.
Date of
order/
proceedings.
Order with signature of Judge and that of parties or
counsel, where necessary.
17 .08.2026  Mr. Majeed Akbar Lodhi, Advocate for t he petitioners.
Rana Ghulam Hussain, Assistant Attorney General for
Pakistan with M. Zeeshan Riaz, S.H.O, F.I.A.
Through  instant  petition ,  the  petitioners,
namely Muhammad Akhtar and Muhammad Ansar, seek
post - arrest  bail  in  FIR  No.  12/26  dated  04.07. 2026,
registered  under Sections 409, 420, and  109 PPC at
Police Station FIA, District D.G. Khan .
2 .  The allegations against the petitioners, namely
Muhammad Akhtar and Muhammad Ansar (petitioners ) ,
in the FIR and from the evidentiary material collected
during the course of the investigation produced before
the Court, are that they both were involved in  receiving
various amounts from the beneficiaries of the Ehsaas
Kafalat  Program  (BISP  Scheme)  for  the  release  of
amounts in their name s , and were also involved with an
HBL Konnect biometric device and an Infinix mobile
phone,  retaining  the  Computerized  Nati onal  Identity
Cards (CNICs) of the beneficiaries.
3 .  I  have  heard  the  arguments  of  the  learned
counsel for the petitioners, the learned Assistant Attorney
General  for  Pakistan  in  the  presence  of  the
complainant/investigating officer, and perused the record
with their able assistance.
4 .  The  petition  in  ha nd  is  a  post - arrest  bail
petition,  and  only  a  tentative  assessment  of  the
evidentiary material produced before the Court can be
made at this stage.  In the FIR itself, it  is mentioned that
Crl.Misc.No.5827 - B of 2026  2
“ at the time of raid, two agents standing inside Ansar
Mobile who we re intercepted were taking CNICs of the
beneficiaries and a commission amount of  Rs. 1,000 from
ladies who came there to receive their amount) from each
of the beneficiaries of the Ehsaas Kafalat Program (BISP
Scheme ” .  No allegations have been made against the
petitioners that they were entrusted with any amount by
any of the beneficiaries and that they then dishonestly
misappropriated the same. When there is no allegation
that  they  were  entrusted  with  any  amount  by  the
beneficiaries, and the allegations are merely that they
retained  Rs. 1,000 out of the total amount which they
handed over to each beneficiary of the Ehsaas Kafalat
Program (BISP Scheme), then the case of the petitioners
becomes one of cheating and not crim inal breach of trust.
Obviously, the offense of cheating is bailable in nature as
per Schedule II of the Code of Criminal Procedure, 1898.
Therefore,  an  outward  effort  has  been  made  by  the
investigating officer of the case to convert the offense of
cheating into criminal breac h of trust, disregarding the
narrative of the FIR as well as the statements recorded of
the witnesses.  In this backdrop, the culpability of the
petitioners as to whether they were privy to the crime
would be determined by the learned trial court after the
recording of evidence.  The petitioners were arrested in
the case on  04.07.2026  and have since been behind  the
bars. Based on the particular facts and circumstances of
the  case,  where  there  are  no  allegations  that  the
petitioners would abscond or interfere with the witnesses,
ke eping the petitioners behind bars would serve no useful
purpose.  It  is  not  the  prosecution's  case  that  the
petitioners are withholding any official record ,  or that the
agents of a bank in whose name any machine/biometric
apparatus was issued are withholdin g it.  The relevant
record and the evidence collected during the investigation
are  with  the  prosecution,  and  therefore,  there  is  no
Crl.Misc.No.5827 - B of 2026  3
question of tampering with the evidence. For what has
been discussed above, case of the petitioners becomes
one  of  further  in quiry  under  Section  497 (2) Cr.P.C.
Liability  of  petitioners  for  the  offences  would  be
determined by the learned trial court after sifting the
evidentiary worth of the material produced before the
same. Reliance in this regard in place on “Saeed Ahmed
vs. Th e State” (1996 SCMR 1132) wherein it has been
held as under: -
“The  learned  counsel  for  the  petitioner
contended  that  there  is  no  prohibition  for
grant of bail in respect of offences mentioned
above,  but  with  malafide  intention
subsequently offence under se ction 409  PPC .
The case entirely depends upon documentary
evidence which seems  to be in possession of
the prosecution and challan has already been
submitted.  The  objection  of  the  learned
counsel  regarding  addition  of section  409,
PPC  may  carry  some  weight  while
considering the bail, application. As there is
no possibility of tampering with the evidence,
which is entirely documentary in nature and in
possession  of  the  prosecution,  in  the
circumstances, we convert the petition into an
appeal and allow it, and  grant bail to the
petitioner on furnishing one surety in the sum
of  Rs.50,000/ -  to  the  satisfaction  of  the
Deputy Registrar, Supreme Court, Lahore.”
Reliance  is  also  placed  on the  case  of “Muhammad
Nawaz vs. The State through Chairman, NAB, Islamabad
and  another” (PLD 2008 SC 438). It has been observed
by the  Honorable  Supreme Court of Pakistan  in the case
of  Manzoo r and 4 others versus The State  (PLD 1972
SC 81)  as under: -
“ It is important to remember that bail is not to be
withheld as a punishment there is no legal or
moral compulsion to keep people in jail merely
on  the  allegation  that  they  have  committed
offences punishable with death or transportation,
unless  reasonable  g rounds  appear  to  exist  to
disclose their complicity. ”
Crl.Misc.No.5827 - B of 2026  4
In the reported case “ Muhammad Saeed Mehdi vs. The
State and 2 others ” (2002 SCMR 282), it was observed
that bail cannot be withheld as punishment in cases of
non - bailable offence against the accused a nd that the
basic  idea  was  to enable  the  accused  to answer the
criminal prosecution against him rather than to make him
not  behind  bars.  In  the  same  case,  the  apex  Court
observed that truth or otherwise of the allegation could
only be determined at the tri al after analysis of evidence
that might be adduced by the parties.  Further reliance is
place  upon the  case  reported  as  Muhammad  Sarfraz
Ansari v.  The State and others  ( PLD  2021 Supreme
Court 738 ) , wherein Honorable Supreme Court held as
under:
“ We are ful ly cognizant of the  well - settled  principle that
at  the  bail  stage  the  court  is  not  to  make  deeper
examination and appreciation  of the evidence collected
during investigation  or to conduct  anything in the nature
of preliminary trial to determine the accused ’s guilt or
innocence. ”
5 .  For what  has been discussed above, this  petition
is accepted and petitioners are admitted to post - arrest bail
subject to their furnishing bail bonds in the sum of
Rs.1,00,000/ -  (one lac only) each with two sureties each
in t he like amount  to the satisfaction of the learned trial
court.
6 .  It is clarified that the observations enumerated
are absolutely tentative in nature and restricted only to
the extent of this particular petition, having no nexus and
relevance with the tr ial, which shall be concluded quite
independently and purely on merit.
(SYED FARHAD ALI SHAH)
JUDGE
Approved for reporting .
JUDGE
Subhani *

[SECTION: LHC_CASE_027]
Case: Crl. Misc. 46375/26 (Masood Aslam Vs The State etc).
Court: Lahore High Court, Lahore. Case No: Crl. Misc. 46375/26. Title: Crl. Misc. 46375/26 (Masood Aslam Vs The State etc). Official Ref: 2026LHC5260. Author Judge: Mr. Justice Muhammad Amjad Pervaiz. Judgment Date (as displayed): 18-08-2026. Record Type: FULL_TEXT_VERBATIM (complete judgment text extracted from the official court PDF). Source URL: https://sys.lhc.gov.pk/appjudgments/2026LHC5260.pdf. Verified: 2026-09-06 against the official court repository.

VERBATIM JUDGMENT TEXT (extracted from the official PDF at the Source URL; 4 pages; spelling, punctuation and lineation as in the PDF):

Form No: HCJD/C - 121
ORDER SHEET
IN THE LAHORE HIGH COURT,  LAHORE
( JUDICIAL DEPARTMENT )
Case No.  Crl. Misc. No. 46375 - CB /20 26
Masood Aslam  Versus  The State, etc.
S r .  No.  of  order /
Proceedings
Date of order /
Proceedings
Order with signatures of Judge, and that of parties or counsel,
where necessary.
12.08.202 6  Ch. Tariq Mehmood,  Advocate  for  the  petitioner.
=====
This criminal miscellaneous has been filed
in  terms  of  Section  497(5)  Cr.P.C.  by  the
petitioner/complainant Masood Aslam for seeking
cancellation  of  pre-arrest  bail  granted  to
respondent  No.2  vide  order  dated  30.06.2026
passed by learned Addl. Sessions Judge, Lahore in
case  FIR  No.506/2026  dated  14.05.2026,  for
offence  under  Section  406  PPC,  registered  at
Police Station, Mozang Lahore.
2.  As per contents of the FIR, the prosecution
story precisely is that the respondent borrowed car
bearing  registration  No.KY-4443  from  the
complainant on 28.11.2025 for ten days in order to
attend a marriage ceremony and later on, he did
not return the same. He initially lingered on the
matter for one pretext or another and ultimately
refused to return it.
3.  Arguments heard. Record perused.
4.  For ready reference, the operative paragraph
of the order impugned is reproduced as under:-
‘ a) Alongwith police record, as well as bail petition
of  the  petitioner,  attested  copies  of  suit  for
declaration,  specific  performance  with  permanent
injunction  along  with  consequential  relief,  titled
"Muhammad Akram Khokhar Vs. Masood Aslam &
another", alongwith order sheet of the Learned Civil
court, regarding subject vehicle is attached according
Crl. Misc. No.46375-CB/2026  2
to which petitioner filed the said suit on 18.02.2026,
with the assertions that he purchased the subject
vehicle from the complainant against the price of
Rs.75,00,000/- and possession was also delivered to
him, in the said suit, complainant appeared before
Learned Civil court on 05.03.2026 and requested
adjournment for written statement and written reply,
on 21.04.2026, ex-parte proceedings were initiated
against the complainant, on 11.05.2026, complainant
filed  application  for  setting  aside  the  ex-parte
proceedings, which was accepted on the statement of
learned counsel for the plaintiff of the said suit,
complainant filed the written statement in the suit on
11.05.2026, while taking the stance that petitioner
borrowed  the  subject  vehicle  for  ten  days  for
attending  family  function  at  Sargodha,  but
fraudulently retained the vehicle.
(b)  The above referred two different versions of the
parties, to be determined by the learned Trial Court,
during the trial regarding the constitution of offence
of criminal breach of trust U/s 406 PPC.
c) Complainant didn't get register the FIR of this
case, after ten days from 28.11.2025, rather he got
registered the instant FIR on 15.05.2026, after delay
of about more than six months, that too after the
submission of written statement in the above referred
civil suit, which was submitted on 11.05.2026.  It
means that the complainant had got registered the
instant FIR after due deliberation, consultation and
after getting legal advice.  This fact is creating a
doubt in the case of complainant, regarding which
petitioner is entitled for benefit of doubt, at bail stage.
d)  Malice  and  malafide  of  the  complainant
could not rule out that he implicated the petitioner for
some ulterior motive, as a counter blast of the above
referred civil suit.
e)  All the above referred facts are establishing
case  of  further  inquiry,  at  pre-arrest  bail  stage,
regarding the involvement of the petitioner in this
case.’
Crl. Misc. No.46375-CB/2026  3
5.  The considerations for the grant of bail and
for  cancellation  of  the  same  are  altogether
different.  Once the bail is granted by a Court of
competent jurisdiction, then strong and exceptional
grounds  would  be  required  for  cancellation
thereof.
Sami  Ullah ’s
case  has  articulated  the
grounds  to  invoke  and  exercise  jurisdiction  in
terms of the Section 497(5) Cr. PC. as under;  Para
5…..
i)  If  the  bail  granting  order  is  patently  illegal,
erroneous, factually incorrect and has resulted into
miscarriage of justice.
ii) That the accused has misused the concession of bail
in any manner.
iii)  That  accused  has  tried  to  hamper  prosecution
evidence  by  persuading/pressurizing  prosecution
witnesses.
iv) That there is likelihood of absconsion of the accused
beyond the jurisdiction of court.
v) That the accused has attempted to interfere with the
smooth course of investigation.
vi) That accused misused his liberty while indulging
into similar offence.
vii)  That  some  fresh  facts  and  material  has  been
collected during the course of investigation with tends
to establish guilt of the accused. ’
6.  The  above  view  is  being  consistently
followed, in  addition  thereof,  it  has  also  been
enunciated  in  Rab  Nawaz’s
case  that  the
principles evolved for examining the bail granting
order for purposes of its cancellation are that the
Court  shall  interfere  on  two  grounds  i)  the
impugned order is perverse on the face of it and
PLD 1995 SC 34
2020 SCMR 1115
2025 SCMR 1357
Crl. Misc. No.46375-CB/2026  4
(ii) the case has been decided in clear disregard of
the settled principles of law .
7.  Learned counsel for the petitioner was heard
at great length, who argued his cause well but
could not demonstrate that any of the grounds
mentioned above are attracted in this case. The
order impugned is neither perverse nor factually
incorrect, nor is  it in  disregard of any settled
principle of law.
8.  In view of the foregoing reasons, this Court
is  not  inclined  to  interfere with  the impugned
order, and thus, the petition in hand, being bereft
of merit, is hereby  dismissed in limine .
( Muhammad Amjad Pervaiz )
Judge
Approved for  R eporting .

Asif *

0 6 .0 8 . 202 6  Mr. Nadeem Akram Wahla,  Advocate for the petitioner.
Through  this  Constitutional  Petition  filed  under
Article 199 of the Constitution of the Islamic Republic of
Pakistan, 1973, the petitioner has called in question the legality
and propriety of the order dated 02.10.2025 passed by the
learned Special Judge Rent/learned Rent Controller, whereby an
application  filed  by  respondent  No.3  under  Section  12(2),
C.P.C. was allowed, the ex - parte  ejectment order along with
memo  of  costs  dated  31.01.2024  was  set  aside  and  the
ejectment petition was restored to its original position with a
direction to the applicants therein to file an amended ejectment
petition by incorporating the name of respondent No.3 in the
memo of parties. The petitioner has also assailed the judgment
dated  18.05.2026  passed  by  the  learned  R evisional  Court,
whereby  the  revision  petition  filed  by  the  petitioner  and
respondent No.4 was dismissed.
2.  The impugned orders have been assailed,  inter alia ,
on the grounds that the learned Courts below misread , non - read
the record  and failed to appreciate the material available on
record,  exercised  jurisdiction  not  vested  in  them,  and
committed  material  irregularity  in  allowing  an  application
under  Section  12(2),  C.P.C.  in  proceedings  governed  by
the  Punjab  Rented  Premises  Act,  2009.  It  has  further
been  contended  that  the  ejectment  order  dated
31.01.2024  h ad  already  attained  finality  and  had
Writ Petition No.4596 of 2026  - 2 -
been executed through delivery of possession, and, therefore, the learned
Rent Controller had no lawful authority to recall the same.
3 .  I have heard the learned counsel for the petitioner at the
preliminary stage and have carefully examined the available record with
his able assistance.
4 .  At the outset, it may be observed that the controversy is
required  to  be  examined  in  the  backdrop  of  the  limited  scope  of
Constitutional  jurisdiction  as  well  as  the  nature  and  extent  of  the
jurisdiction  exercised  by  a  Rent  Controller  while  dealing  with  an
allegation that an order of ejectment has been procured through fraud,
misrepresentation  and  concealment of material facts.
5.  The record shows that the petitioner along with  respondents
No.4 and 5  instituted the ejectment petition against respondent No.6  and
procured a final eviction order dated 31.01.2024.  However, said order
was assailed by respondent No.3 through moving  an  application under
Section 12(2), C.P.C. , which was allowed on 02.10.2025 and resultantly
order dated 31.01.2024 was set - aside.  The order dated 02.10.2025 was
challenged by the petitioner and respondent No.4 through a revision
petition ,  dismissed on 18.05.2026 , whereas respondent No.5 did not
challenge the same. Respondent No.4 has also not joined the present
proceedings. Consequently, the impugned orders have attained finality to
the extent of  resp o ndents No.4 and 5 . The controversy before this Court,
therefore, survives only at the instance of the petitioner.
6 .  The  facts,  in  brief,  are  that  the  petitioner  along  with
respondents No.4 and 5  (hereinafter referred to as the applicants)  filed an
ejectment petition on 31.07.2023 against respondent No.6 in respect of a
property situated at Mohallah Banigran, Mouza Bahawalpur, measur ing
18 marlas. The case set - up in the ejectment petition was that respondent
No.6 had obtained possession of the premises as a tenant pursuant to a
tenancy/lease agreement dated 02.02.2022 at a monthly rent of Rs.5,000/ -
for a period of one year and had subsequently failed to pay rent and hand
over possession despite demand and notice.  The applicants claimed
ownership over the property in different shares. According to them, the
Writ Petition No.4596 of 2026  - 3 -
petitioner and respondent No.4 were owners of 5 marlas each, whereas
the remaining portion was claimed by respondent No.5 and her husband.
Despite service, publication and opportunities, respondent No.6 did not
appear before the learned Rent  Controller  was proceeded against ex - parte
on 07.10.2023. The  petitioner and  the  applicants  produced their evidence,
including respondent No.4 as AW - 1, his affidavit as Exh.A - 1, Ghulam
Nabi and Muhammad Mushtaq as attesting witnesses  of rent agreement
as AW - 2 and AW - 3 along with their affidavits as Exh.A - 4 and Exh.A - 5,
the tenancy agreement as Exh.A - 2 and copy of the record of rights for the
year 2019 - 20 as Exh.A - 3.  On the basis of the ex - parte evidence, the
learned Rent  Controller  allowed the ejectment petition and passed the
final  eviction order along with memo of costs dated 31.01.2024. In
execution of the said order, possession of the disputed premises was
delivered to the applicants.
7.  The controversy, however, did not end with the delivery of
possession. Respondent No.3 thereafter approached the learned Rent
Controller through an application under Section 12(2), C.P.C., asserting
that  the  ejectment  order  had  been  procured  by  misdescription  and
concealment  of  the  identity  and  ownership  of  the  property.  He
maintained that the premises from which possession had been obtained
did not belong to the applicants but formed part of property owned by
him and situated in Khata  Nos.36/32 and 38/34. He relied upon registered
sale deed No.780 dated 19.03.1986 and asserted that the question of his
title had already been adjudicated in his favour up to the Supreme Court
of Pakistan.  The  applicants  contested the application and maintained that
the disputed premises belonged to them and that possession had lawfully
been delivered in execution of the ejectment order. Respondent No.6,
who had remained absent throughout the original ejectment proceedings,
also appeared at that stage and submitted a written reply supporting the
applicants.
8.  During the proceedings, it became apparent that the real
controversy between the parties was not merely one of tenancy but
concerned  the  identity  and  demarcation  of  the  property  in  respect
Writ Petition No.4596 of 2026  - 4 -
whereof the ejectment order had been obtained and executed. The parties,
therefore,  expressed  consensus  that  the question  of  demarcation  be
determined through a Local Commission.  Consequently, vide order dated
02.10.2024, the learned Rent  Controller  appointed the Tehsildar, Saddar,
Bahawalpur, as Local Commission with directions to demarcate the
disputed  property  and  submit  his  report.  The  Local  Commission
conducted demarcation proceedings in the presence of the parties. The
parties agreed to have the property identified through Global Positioning
System (GPS). Muhammad Waseem son of Sanaullah, an expert in GPS
technology, carried out the demarcation in the presence of the parties and
prepared a site plan.  The relevant portion of the said report is as under: -
"رفنیقیاکومفقامستعایکایگ۔اوررہدورفنیقییکرطفےسشیپرکدہراکی ر ڈالمہظحایکایگ۔دعبذہاالمہظح

ہکفلذہاےہ۔ "
According to the report, the property actually found on the spot
measured approximately 13.4 marlas  and was situated in Rectangle
No.144/4, Killa No.21. The report thus materially differed from the
description and extent of the property given in the ejectment proceedings.
The Local Commission further recorded that the land so identified was
not comprised in the ownership of the  applicants  in the manner asserted
by them. The report also referred to the relevant revenue record and
concluded that although the identified land did not stand recorded in the
name of respondent No.3 in the manner alleged by him, the same was
traceable to the ownership of his mother, Mst. Manzoor Masood, and had
subsequently  come into the possession of respondent No.3  through
Writ Petition No.4596 of 2026  - 5 -
private partition  and before delivery of possession to the applicants
through warrant of possession it was in the possession of respondent
No.3 .  The Local Commission supported its conclusions through revenue
material, site plan and the result of the physical demarcation.  The
relevant part of the said conclusion is as under: -
" (1) ربہبق  م
